Not ready to execute. I read all three files in full and checked the Dream-RSI paper, implementation, and cited business LOOP artifacts. No files were edited. Reviewed snapshots: PLAN `6b8fc5df6d36`, GOAL `21066a1a0a61`, specification `2814f8f3edcd`.

1. **Critical — [PLAN Phase 1.2 and §10](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:118): outcome leakage is incorrectly repairable by disclosure.** The plan permits external-outcome-informed corrections to remain with “transductive” wording. Specification §5 explicitly prohibits this: only covariate-only joint preprocessing qualifies for that treatment. This can falsely satisfy C3, C4, and C6. **Fix:** require development-only reconstruction or genuinely independent validation; otherwise retain the unmet independence gate. Audit outcome-guided target selection and model selection as well as normalization.

2. **High — [PLAN Phase 1.3–1.4 versus Phase 2.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:119): final-outcome protection starts too late.** Phase 1 calls for outcomes, dynamic ranges, missingness, and QC across sources; partitions and access guards appear in Phase 2. An executor must invent which Sanger values may be inspected. **Fix:** establish provisional metadata-based partitions and quarantine before outcome-bearing imports. Define permitted metadata, independent QC controls, and exposure logging explicitly.

3. **High — [PLAN §0.4.2–0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:46): global catalog inputs conflict with prefix-only replay.** Catalogs, frontiers, summaries, and priority scores summarize completed attempts, including outcomes a replay episode has not revealed. Passing these current objects into replay leaks future rewards indirectly. **Fix:** construct episode-specific views containing only admissible earlier-world history and the revealed current-world prefix. Test that changing hidden outcomes cannot change policy inputs or decisions.

4. **High — [PLAN §0.3, §0.4.1, §0.4.4 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:56): recombination is incompatible with the stated tree schema.** Nodes otherwise have one parent and belong to root-to-leaf chains; recombination introduces multiple parents without defining replay eligibility, world membership, or ancestry costs. Parent existence alone is insufficient. **Fix:** define ordered parent arrays, acyclicity, compatible study/evaluator versions, cross-world dependencies, and immutable contributions. Require every information-bearing parent to be revealed or explicitly available initially. Charge the union of required ancestry once.

5. **High — [PLAN §0.4.4 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:53): “exact context” has no operational definition.** Generation depends on changing catalogs, summaries, frontiers, budget, and rejection history. Matching a parent ID and an unspecified context hash does not establish counterfactual validity. **Fix:** freeze and retain a canonical manifest of every actual generator input, including all parent artifacts and history-derived inputs. Either make refinement parent-local or mark reordered generations unsupported whenever any relevant input differs.

6. **High — [PLAN §0.4.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:63): historical pruning events cannot define a challenger’s counterfactual legal actions.** Reordered observations can produce different statuses and pruning times. Replaying the original legal-action sequence either leaks future information or constrains the challenger using another policy’s history. **Fix:** recompute legal actions from the challenger’s revealed prefix using frozen transition rules; preserve historical decisions for auditing, not as counterfactual state.

7. **High — [PLAN §0.4.6 and Phase 4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:65): common-support screening is underspecified.** There is no definition of coverage, minimum usable support, unsupported-action termination, or whether policies are compared on identical episodes. Selectively dropping unsupported continuations can make a policy appear efficient. **Fix:** freeze the comparison set, coverage denominator, eligibility threshold, unsupported-episode treatment, aggregation weights, and fresh-rollout fallback rule. Unsupported actions must never become successful cheap stops.

8. **High — [PLAN §0.4.3 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:49): the replay objective is not executable.** “Area-over-the-curve,” robustness, and the adaptation of Equation 1 lack formulas, scales, horizons, normalization, and tie rules. Furthermore, the paper describes ρ as a budget-control parameter, rather than the stated interpolation of objective emphasis. **Fix:** specify the complete mathematical objective and ρ interface before Lock 1, including signed/lower-is-better endpoints, invalid results, measured costs, and zero-attainment cases. [Dream-RSI §3](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.14858v2#S3).

9. **High — [PLAN §0.4.5, §0.5.3 and §0.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:92): the effective generator can change outside the policy module.** LLM ranking, catalog extraction, guidance, repairs, and unstick recommendations influence which candidates exist and run. Versioned selection code alone does not isolate policy improvement. **Fix:** freeze the whole proposal/guidance/extraction protocol during each comparison and apply the same protocol to fixed arms using their own admissible histories. Record discretionary interventions as protocol changes or excluded experiments.

10. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:46): catalog status rules are incomplete.** Nine statuses receive only two example predicates. Their precedence, missing-evidence behavior, recovery transitions, and conflict resolution are absent; schema validation does not verify factual classification. **Fix:** require a total transition table at Lock 1, with evidence references and deterministic predicates. Separate exploration depth, promise, fragility, and recoverability into independent fields where appropriate.

11. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.4 and §0.5.1](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:54): ordinary refinement has no legal origin.** A successful, sufficiently explored direction undergoing a single-parent refinement is neither `new`, `underexplored`, `combination`, nor `repair`. Winner mutations therefore require false classifications. **Fix:** add an explicit refinement/mutation origin with parent requirements, or define origins at branch creation and a separate node-operation taxonomy.

12. **High — [PLAN §0.4.4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:58): context hashes are the wrong deduplication key.** Different artifacts can arise from identical contexts; identical configurations can recur under different contexts. The rule can reject legitimate repairs while counting repeated analyses as new trials. **Fix:** distinguish attempt IDs, generation-context hashes, artifact/configuration identities, and evaluation identities. Define authorized replication and repair exceptions explicitly.

13. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.3 and §0.4.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:50): frontier retention and pruning rules conflict.** A saturated trajectory can remain nondominated, making it simultaneously “kept refinable” and legally pruned. Cross-direction quality and robustness are also not necessarily comparable. **Fix:** define precedence and comparable scoring domains. Treat computational saturation as a resource-allocation judgment, not evidence that a scientific family is exhausted.

14. **High — [PLAN §0.5.1 and §0.5.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:76): screening does not justify exhaustive negative verdicts.** Battery-testing every screen-positive leaves approximate-screen false negatives untested. A failed cheap diagnostic also need not falsify every member of a family. Consequently, `DEAD`, `CLOSED`, or “no candidate passes” can overstate the evidence. **Fix:** require a demonstrated necessary-condition screen for exhaustive rejection, battery every relevant candidate, or scope the verdict to tested finalists. Preserve untested members explicitly.

15. **Medium — [PLAN §0.5.1 versus Phase 4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:78): the generation protocol mixes scientific artifacts and policy challengers.** It requires every screen-positive to receive a battery, whereas Phase 4 fresh-tests the selected challenger. Candidate types, screen-positive rules, and which battery applies are unspecified. **Fix:** define separate artifact and policy lifecycles, selection rules, and denominators. Reserve battery resources before screening; label unfinished batteries pending when ceilings intervene.

16. **Medium — [PLAN §0.5.1.8](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:81): trial accounting is too narrow.** Counting screened configurations omits policy rewrites, budget-preference evaluations, diagnostics, repaired revisions, battery-only evaluations, and repeated inspection. A single total also obscures scientific versus engineering adaptation. **Fix:** maintain separate cumulative counts for unique configurations, attempts, hypothesis inspections, policy candidates, replay episodes, batteries, and replications. None substitutes for a valid confirmatory procedure.

17. **High — [PLAN §0.5.2 and §8](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:84): an unchanged anchor cannot certify an unchanged evaluator.** Evaluator changes can preserve one baseline while altering candidate rankings or validity decisions. Keeping versions side by side does not make their scores poolable. **Fix:** prohibit mixed evaluator versions within comparisons and replay pools. Require explicit compatibility checks and invalidate affected results; substantive changes require the new-study/new-evidence procedure.

18. **High — [PLAN Phase 4.3–4.4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:156): promotion need not beat the retained incumbent in fresh rollouts.** A challenger can beat fixed exploration while performing worse than the current incumbent. Replay superiority cannot establish fresh-rollout superiority because replay is explicitly only a screen. **Fix:** include the incumbent in fresh paired comparisons and freeze the required improvement or noninferiority rule before selection.

19. **High — [PLAN §0.4.6 and Phases 4–5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:65): end-to-end cost allocation is undefined.** Shared E0, cached artifacts, ancestors, summaries, extraction, reviews, unstick calls, and learning overhead across multiple reserved panels lack charging rules. Copying history read-only does not make its production free. Arbitrary amortization can manufacture C9 savings. **Fix:** freeze campaign-level allocation, deployment count, shared-cost treatment, and every charged component; reconcile reported totals directly against the resource ledger.

20. **High — [PLAN Phase 2.4 and Phase 5.2–5.3; GOAL ambition 5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:167): the primary resource endpoint remains ambiguous.** “Total measured cost” does not identify a resource or a valid scalarization. The alternative-endpoint rule omits explicit requirements to justify its practical margin and uncertainty criterion and measure or strictly match other material resources. **Fix:** require named units, aggregation, margin, uncertainty method, and matching tolerances in Lock 1. Preserve the existing unresolved-host-cost gate.

21. **High — [PLAN Phase 5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:166): the policy comparison lacks an inferential design.** There is no required number of independent evaluation blocks, savings estimand, paired estimator, censoring-compatible interval procedure, or precision feasibility check. Repeated seeds and correlated gene panels cannot supply missing independent tasks. **Fix:** make these mandatory Lock-1 fields, assess achievable precision before optimization, and explicitly bound transfer from larger engineering task cohorts to the seven-model environmental problem.

22. **High — [PLAN §0.6 and Phases 5–6; GOAL autonomy](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:98): failed reserved-policy evaluation has no consumption rule.** Unmet gates become further mandates, but Layer-B results can then guide revisions and another purported reserved comparison. “Development histories only” does not prevent indirect use through global summaries and reviews. **Fix:** mark B panels consumed after evaluation. Further adaptation requires new independent B evidence for another confirmatory policy claim.

23. **High — [PLAN Phase 6.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:177): adaptively reused environmental evidence lacks an explicit inferential status.** The plan correctly distinguishes transfer confirmation from environmental validation, but omits the specification’s requirement to retain reused environmental evidence as exploratory or justified selection-adjusted evidence. External transfer success plus Holm correction cannot retroactively confirm the selected environmental effect. **Fix:** attach component-level evidence status and adjustment/independence requirements to every final claim and atlas decision.

24. **High — [PLAN Phase 2.1 and Phase 3.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:130): joint environmental uncertainty is weakened to generic uncertainty propagation.** The specification explicitly requires shared-model dependence to survive downstream analysis. Independent per-gene error bars or simulations would miss covariance induced by the same seven models, repeated contrasts, and relevant target/pathway dependence. **Fix:** require joint resampling or an explicit covariance model and demonstrate that the entire downstream transfer analysis propagates it.

25. **High — [PLAN Phase 6.3; GOAL C10](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:178): meaningful dependency strength is gated only for invariance claims.** Specification §6 requires meaningful dependency in environments covered by any positive vulnerability claim. A conditional environmental effect can be statistically clear while both conditions show negligible dependency. **Fix:** predeclare and test meaningful dependency-strength requirements for every positive vulnerability claim, independently of its environmental-change criterion.

26. **Medium — [PLAN Phase 2.3](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:132): “direction flips” are ambiguously invalidated.** An assay-orientation bug is invalid; a correctly measured reversal is potentially a valuable refutation or heterogeneity result. The blanket wording permits rejection of inconvenient findings. **Fix:** distinguish verified orientation/preprocessing errors from valid opposite-direction biological results. Only the former invalidate an analysis.

27. **High — [PLAN §0.6 versus §0.5.2 and §10; GOAL autonomy versus terminal states](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:96): autonomy rules prevent legitimate inconclusive termination.** Failed gates trigger repeated consultations and then another audit or literature check indefinitely. This conflicts with negative-EV stopping, honest non-complete outcomes, and specification §14’s prohibition on indefinite retries. Only the first pilot has a numeric wall ceiling. **Fix:** define campaign allocations, global retry limits, no-progress criteria, and admissible non-complete terminal determinations that do not require success or exhausting every resource.

28. **Medium — [PLAN §0.6 and GOAL autonomy](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:98): terminal reporting depends on a call that may be unavailable or unaffordable.** Sol-xhigh must certify even budget exhaustion and hard blockage, potentially after the dispatch budget or runtime has ended. **Fix:** reserve bounded terminal-review resources beforehand and permit immediate mechanical checkpointing and honest exhausted/blocked reporting if that review cannot run. Positive completion must still require its mandated review.

29. **High — [PLAN §0.1–0.3 and §8; GOAL model routing](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:32): hashes substitute for missing replay provenance.** The required node fields omit explicit selecting-policy version, actual action input, catalog/summary versions, and retained rendered prompts. A prompt/context hash alone cannot reconstruct or audit its contents. **Fix:** retain the exact prompt and input artifacts, action/observation event sequence, policy state/version, and execution environment manifest; make those dependencies immutable and addressable.

30. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.4 versus GOAL model routing](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:53): the “full tried history” promise exceeds the defined proposer interface.** PLAN enumerates catalog, summary, frontier, budget, and locked spaces; GOAL requires the full tried history and generation ledger. Aggregates can omit decisive failures and contradictions. **Fix:** define one consistent context/retrieval contract, an exhaustive history index, provenance for retrieved evidence, and deterministic handling of context limits.

31. **Medium — [PLAN §0.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:28): reservation accounting omits required worst-case dispatch constraints.** “Reserve before dispatch” does not specify a conservative billable maximum, concurrent reservations, retry reservations, or rejection of providers whose maximum cannot be bounded. Estimated-cost reservations can overspend a configured cap. **Fix:** reproduce specification §4’s constraints explicitly in the wrapper acceptance criteria and test concurrent requests, retries, and interrupted reservations.

32. **Medium — [PLAN G3 and §9; GOAL definition of done](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:147): the first substantive report precedes the first policy-improvement cycle.** G3 contains fixed-policy observations and replay construction, yet the report promises a retained/promoted policy result. This can present an unchanged starting policy as a tested retention decision. **Fix:** label G3 an interim pilot report with policy comparison explicitly unperformed; issue the required cycle report after the first fresh comparison or its documented blocker. G3 alone cannot establish C13.

33. **Medium — [PLAN Phase 6.1 versus Phase 7](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:176): the final novelty audit occurs at the wrong milestone.** The specification requires another audit before manuscript finalization. Lock 2 may substantially precede that point, allowing C1/C6 to be certified using stale novelty evidence. **Fix:** add a dated claim-specific novelty refresh to G7 and terminal determination, without using it to revise consumed confirmatory tests.

34. **Low — [PLAN §0.1 and Phase 2.4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:22): §7.3 does not exist.** Both cost-measurability and endpoint-selection instructions point to this missing section. These references concern a completion-critical gate. **Fix:** point to the actual Phase 5 provisions or create a definitive cost-method section.

35. **Low — [GOAL non-negotiables](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/GOAL.md:35): “all computation on this laptop” conflicts with mandated hosted-model calls.** The specification distinguishes local scientific computation from host-provided frontier reasoning. The literal GOAL wording makes authorized execution internally inconsistent. **Fix:** require all scientific fitting, simulation, evaluation, and data processing locally, while explicitly permitting the authorized hosted reasoning interface.

Not ready to execute. I read all three files in full and checked the Dream-RSI paper, implementation, and cited business LOOP artifacts. No files were edited. Reviewed snapshots: PLAN `6b8fc5df6d36`, GOAL `21066a1a0a61`, specification `2814f8f3edcd`.

1. **Critical — [PLAN Phase 1.2 and §10](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:118): outcome leakage is incorrectly repairable by disclosure.** The plan permits external-outcome-informed corrections to remain with “transductive” wording. Specification §5 explicitly prohibits this: only covariate-only joint preprocessing qualifies for that treatment. This can falsely satisfy C3, C4, and C6. **Fix:** require development-only reconstruction or genuinely independent validation; otherwise retain the unmet independence gate. Audit outcome-guided target selection and model selection as well as normalization.

2. **High — [PLAN Phase 1.3–1.4 versus Phase 2.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:119): final-outcome protection starts too late.** Phase 1 calls for outcomes, dynamic ranges, missingness, and QC across sources; partitions and access guards appear in Phase 2. An executor must invent which Sanger values may be inspected. **Fix:** establish provisional metadata-based partitions and quarantine before outcome-bearing imports. Define permitted metadata, independent QC controls, and exposure logging explicitly.

3. **High — [PLAN §0.4.2–0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:46): global catalog inputs conflict with prefix-only replay.** Catalogs, frontiers, summaries, and priority scores summarize completed attempts, including outcomes a replay episode has not revealed. Passing these current objects into replay leaks future rewards indirectly. **Fix:** construct episode-specific views containing only admissible earlier-world history and the revealed current-world prefix. Test that changing hidden outcomes cannot change policy inputs or decisions.

4. **High — [PLAN §0.3, §0.4.1, §0.4.4 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:56): recombination is incompatible with the stated tree schema.** Nodes otherwise have one parent and belong to root-to-leaf chains; recombination introduces multiple parents without defining replay eligibility, world membership, or ancestry costs. Parent existence alone is insufficient. **Fix:** define ordered parent arrays, acyclicity, compatible study/evaluator versions, cross-world dependencies, and immutable contributions. Require every information-bearing parent to be revealed or explicitly available initially. Charge the union of required ancestry once.

5. **High — [PLAN §0.4.4 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:53): “exact context” has no operational definition.** Generation depends on changing catalogs, summaries, frontiers, budget, and rejection history. Matching a parent ID and an unspecified context hash does not establish counterfactual validity. **Fix:** freeze and retain a canonical manifest of every actual generator input, including all parent artifacts and history-derived inputs. Either make refinement parent-local or mark reordered generations unsupported whenever any relevant input differs.

6. **High — [PLAN §0.4.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:63): historical pruning events cannot define a challenger’s counterfactual legal actions.** Reordered observations can produce different statuses and pruning times. Replaying the original legal-action sequence either leaks future information or constrains the challenger using another policy’s history. **Fix:** recompute legal actions from the challenger’s revealed prefix using frozen transition rules; preserve historical decisions for auditing, not as counterfactual state.

7. **High — [PLAN §0.4.6 and Phase 4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:65): common-support screening is underspecified.** There is no definition of coverage, minimum usable support, unsupported-action termination, or whether policies are compared on identical episodes. Selectively dropping unsupported continuations can make a policy appear efficient. **Fix:** freeze the comparison set, coverage denominator, eligibility threshold, unsupported-episode treatment, aggregation weights, and fresh-rollout fallback rule. Unsupported actions must never become successful cheap stops.

8. **High — [PLAN §0.4.3 and §0.4.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:49): the replay objective is not executable.** “Area-over-the-curve,” robustness, and the adaptation of Equation 1 lack formulas, scales, horizons, normalization, and tie rules. Furthermore, the paper describes ρ as a budget-control parameter, rather than the stated interpolation of objective emphasis. **Fix:** specify the complete mathematical objective and ρ interface before Lock 1, including signed/lower-is-better endpoints, invalid results, measured costs, and zero-attainment cases. [Dream-RSI §3](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.14858v2#S3).

9. **High — [PLAN §0.4.5, §0.5.3 and §0.6](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:92): the effective generator can change outside the policy module.** LLM ranking, catalog extraction, guidance, repairs, and unstick recommendations influence which candidates exist and run. Versioned selection code alone does not isolate policy improvement. **Fix:** freeze the whole proposal/guidance/extraction protocol during each comparison and apply the same protocol to fixed arms using their own admissible histories. Record discretionary interventions as protocol changes or excluded experiments.

10. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:46): catalog status rules are incomplete.** Nine statuses receive only two example predicates. Their precedence, missing-evidence behavior, recovery transitions, and conflict resolution are absent; schema validation does not verify factual classification. **Fix:** require a total transition table at Lock 1, with evidence references and deterministic predicates. Separate exploration depth, promise, fragility, and recoverability into independent fields where appropriate.

11. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.4 and §0.5.1](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:54): ordinary refinement has no legal origin.** A successful, sufficiently explored direction undergoing a single-parent refinement is neither `new`, `underexplored`, `combination`, nor `repair`. Winner mutations therefore require false classifications. **Fix:** add an explicit refinement/mutation origin with parent requirements, or define origins at branch creation and a separate node-operation taxonomy.

12. **High — [PLAN §0.4.4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:58): context hashes are the wrong deduplication key.** Different artifacts can arise from identical contexts; identical configurations can recur under different contexts. The rule can reject legitimate repairs while counting repeated analyses as new trials. **Fix:** distinguish attempt IDs, generation-context hashes, artifact/configuration identities, and evaluation identities. Define authorized replication and repair exceptions explicitly.

13. **Medium — [PLAN §0.4.3 and §0.4.5](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:50): frontier retention and pruning rules conflict.** A saturated trajectory can remain nondominated, making it simultaneously “kept refinable” and legally pruned. Cross-direction quality and robustness are also not necessarily comparable. **Fix:** define precedence and comparable scoring domains. Treat computational saturation as a resource-allocation judgment, not evidence that a scientific family is exhausted.

14. **High — [PLAN §0.5.1 and §0.5.2](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:76): screening does not justify exhaustive negative verdicts.** Battery-testing every screen-positive leaves approximate-screen false negatives untested. A failed cheap diagnostic also need not falsify every member of a family. Consequently, `DEAD`, `CLOSED`, or “no candidate passes” can overstate the evidence. **Fix:** require a demonstrated necessary-condition screen for exhaustive rejection, battery every relevant candidate, or scope the verdict to tested finalists. Preserve untested members explicitly.

15. **Medium — [PLAN §0.5.1 versus Phase 4](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:78): the generation protocol mixes scientific artifacts and policy challengers.** It requires every screen-positive to receive a battery, whereas Phase 4 fresh-tests the selected challenger. Candidate types, screen-positive rules, and which battery applies are unspecified. **Fix:** define separate artifact and policy lifecycles, selection rules, and denominators. Reserve battery resources before screening; label unfinished batteries pending when ceilings intervene.

16. **Medium — [PLAN §0.5.1.8](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:81): trial accounting is too narrow.** Counting screened configurations omits policy rewrites, budget-preference evaluations, diagnostics, repaired revisions, battery-only evaluations, and repeated inspection. A single total also obscures scientific versus engineering adaptation. **Fix:** maintain separate cumulative counts for unique configurations, attempts, hypothesis inspections, policy candidates, replay episodes, batteries, and replications. None substitutes for a valid confirmatory procedure.

17. **High — [PLAN §0.5.2 and §8](/Users/bmarti44/github/bufferright/research/PLAN.md:84): an unchanged anchor cannot certify an unchanged evaluator.** Evaluator changes can preserve one baseline while altering candidate rankings or validity decisions. Keeping versions side by side does not make their scores poolable. **Fix:** prohibit mixed evaluator versions within comparisons and replay pools. Require explicit compatibility checks and invalidate affected results; substantive changes require the new-study/new-evidence procedure.

     8	Sequencing invariant: **no outcome-bearing import before provisional partitions + quarantine; no adaptive search before identifiability (G1); no policy optimization before the frozen evaluator (G2/Lock 1); no final outcome touched before Lock 2.** Failing a gate narrows the question or ends honestly — never lowers the standard.

    12	## 0. Operating rules (bind every phase)
    13	
    14	### 0.1 Model routing & the frozen generator protocol (user-mandated)
    15	| Role | Model | Effort | Pattern |
    16	|---|---|---|---|
    17	| Adversarial review of **everything** | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `high` | `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort='"high"' -s read-only` |
    18	| Generation (candidates, challengers, summaries, extraction, unstick) | `gpt-6.1-sol` | `xhigh` | same, `xhigh`, sandbox per task |
    19	| Lead investigator / statistics / implementation | Host agent | — | Local; all computation local |
    20	
    21	- **Review protocol:** severity-tagged issues (low/medium/high/critical) with file:line evidence + one 0–100 score → lead verifies each (accept/reject with reason) → fix all verified → re-review until **≥ 90 and zero verified high/critical**. Transcripts + dispositions in `reviews/` + review ledger.
    22	- **Generator-protocol freeze (isolates policy improvement):** for any policy comparison, the *entire* generation protocol is frozen and versioned — proposer prompt, direction-summary prompt, catalog-extraction prompt+schema, candidate ranking inputs, repair/unstick allowances, model IDs + efforts. Fixed arms run the **same protocol on their own admissible histories**. Any discretionary human/agent intervention outside the frozen protocol is logged as a **protocol change** (starts a new comparison epoch) or the affected experiments are **excluded** from the comparison. Only the executable **policy module** differs between arms.
    23	- **Context/retrieval contract:** the proposer receives (a) an **exhaustive history index** (every attempt: ID, direction, origin, parents, outcome class, quality, cost — no omission), (b) the episode-admissible catalog/frontier view (§0.4.2), (c) the current direction summary, (d) retrieved full records for index entries it cites. Retrieval is deterministic (cited IDs only, logged); context-limit handling = index always complete, full records by citation, truncation never silent (overflow ⇒ generation splits by direction). This is the single definition of "full tried history" used in GOAL.md.
    24	- Specialist roles stay distinct (novelty, provenance, modeling, evaluator, adversarial review). The evaluator gets a narrower interface than the proposer — a logged protocol, **not** a cryptographic boundary, never described as one.
    25	- Every model call ledgered: model, effort, exact prompt (retained, §0.3), tokens when exposed, wall/CPU, peak RSS, cost or `unknown`. Additional metered API cap = **$0** unless user-configured; unmeasurable material cost ⇒ C9 all-in savings uncertifiable (Phase 5).
    26	
    27	### 0.2 Resource envelope & worst-case reservations (`config/resources.json`, enforced in code)
    28	- No GPU; ≤ 2 CPU-heavy jobs; research RSS ≤ min(8 GiB, 60% RAM) = **8 GiB**; exploratory timeout **600 s** (longer only via recorded campaign allocation); downloads ≤ **20 GiB**; pilot = 24 nodes + 3 challengers + incumbent, ≤ 8 h; **campaign allocations** (total wall time, node count, model-call count per phase) fixed at Lock 1 within these ceilings. Ceilings never auto-increase.
    29	- **Reservation rules (spec §4, acceptance-tested):** before any dispatch, reserve the **conservative billable maximum** (not an estimate); concurrent reservations and **retry reservations count** against the cap; the reservation record is **persisted+fsynced before dispatch**; interrupted ⇒ stays `reserved` until reconciled against returned usage; **never dispatch to a provider whose billable maximum cannot be bounded** by config; completed calls never repeated on resume. Tests must cover concurrent requests, retries, and interrupted reservations. (Local compute jobs reserve wall/RSS slots the same way with $0 cost.)
    30	
    31	### 0.3 Persistence, provenance retention & honesty primitives
    32	- Append-only JSONL ledgers (`experiments`, `resources`, `reviews`, `claims`); node states `proposed → dispatched → completed|failed → committed`; checkpoint after every completed experiment.
    33	- **Retained provenance (hashes are indexes, not substitutes):** a content-addressed artifact store (`artifacts/sha256/…`) retains, immutably, for every node: artifact bytes; **exact rendered prompt**; **canonical generator-input manifest** (every input artifact ID+hash, incl. parent artifacts, catalog/summary/frontier snapshot IDs, budget state, rejection history shown); **action/observation event sequence** (policy inputs/outputs in order); selecting **policy ID+version+state**; generator model+effort+seed; data/split/evaluator hashes **and versions**; execution-environment manifest (env lock hash, host fingerprint); result, validity diagnostics, failures, measured costs.
    34	- `STATUS.md` + `status.json`: phase, incumbent, spend, three separate status fields (`scientific_discovery`, `prediction_improvement`, `policy_improvement`), unmet C1–C13 gates, next action, exact resume command. "Running" claimed only with live PID/job + heartbeat.
    35	- Terminal states only from GOAL. Failed final test stays failed; reuse of its outcomes = exploratory.
    36	
    37	### 0.4 Trajectory system (Dream-RSI adaptation — departures disclosed)
    38	
    39	Adapted from Dream-RSI (arXiv:2609.14858; `google-research/dream-rsi`): discovery history as replay simulator; offline policy improvement ("dreaming") then online redeployment; fixed generator+evaluator+task, only policy changes. Spec constraints override the paper where they conflict; then the method is labeled **Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation**.
    40	
    41	**0.4.1 Units & DAG schema.** *Node/attempt* = one evaluated artifact. Every node has an **ordered parent array** (possibly empty, one, or many), forming an **acyclic** lineage DAG validated at insert. Each parent edge records the parent's immutable contribution (which components carried over). Nodes carry `study_id`, `evaluator_version`, `world_id` (the round/tree that produced them; recombination children belong to their birth world and reference parents across worlds by ID). **Replay eligibility:** a node is replayable in an episode only if (a) every information-bearing parent is either revealed earlier in the episode or in the initially-available set, (b) study/evaluator versions match the episode, and (c) its canonical input manifest matches the episode state (§0.4.6). **Ancestry cost** = cost of the union of required ancestor nodes, charged **once** per episode (Phase 5 allocation). *Branch/trajectory* = the primary-parent (first-parent) chain view of the DAG, used for best-so-far curves; *discovery tree* = one round's forest under one policy on one panel, frozen+hashed into the **trace pool** as a replay world (pool only grows). Tree shape `BRANCHES × (REFINE+1)` scaled to budget; exact values frozen at Lock 1. *Direction* = coherent research line over many nodes.
    42	
    43	*Identities (dedup done right):* `attempt_id` (each execution) · `context_hash` (generator-input manifest hash) · `config_id` (hash of the artifact's scientific configuration) · `evaluation_id` (config × data × split × evaluator version). Re-running an existing `evaluation_id` is a **replication** (allowed only as a prespecified replication/repair with reason, counted separately); same context but new config = legitimate new attempt; same config new context = duplicate configuration (rejected unless authorized replication). Trial accounting uses these levels (§0.5.1.8).
    44	
    45	**0.4.2 Direction catalog with episode views.** `directions/catalog.json` + immutable snapshots (`history/iterNNN.json`). Status is **four independent fields**, each with deterministic predicates in a **total transition table frozen at Lock 1** (every state combination + missing-evidence + precedence + recovery transitions defined; each transition cites evidence node IDs): `depth` ∈ {unexplored, underexplored, explored, well_explored} · `promise` ∈ {unknown, unpromising, promising} · `fragility` ∈ {unknown, robust, fragile} · `recoverability` ∈ {n/a, recoverable, dead}. Derived labels (e.g. "saturated" = depth well_explored + marginal-gain < ε over last k attempts) are **resource-allocation judgments, never scientific exhaustion verdicts**. Catalog also stores: evidence attempt IDs, best/median quality, quality-vs-cost slope, failure taxonomy, `avoid_repeating`, bottlenecks. Extraction (sol-xhigh, schema-validated) proposes classifications; **deterministic predicates verify them** against recorded outcomes — mismatches rejected+ledgered.
    46	**Episode views (no future leakage):** anything fed to a policy/generator during replay — catalog snapshot, frontier, summaries, priorities, legal actions — is **recomputed from admissible history only**: complete earlier worlds + the revealed prefix of the current world. Global/current catalog objects never enter replay. Acceptance test: mutating any unrevealed outcome must not change any episode-view artifact or policy decision (hidden-outcome invariance test).
    47	
    48	**0.4.3 Trajectory scoring (formal, deterministic, no LLM).** Locked at Lock 1 with formulas; defaults: per-node **valid quality** `q ∈ [0,1]` = locked monotone transform of the primary scientific endpoint (higher = better; sign/direction fixed in contract; invalid ⇒ no q, counted invalid, never 0-as-quality). Per trajectory with cumulative measured cost `c`: best-valid-so-far `B(c) = max q over valid nodes with cumulative cost ≤ c` (0 if none); **cost-to-threshold** `C(τ) = min c with B(c) ≥ τ`, else **censored at budget** (never an invented number); **normalized area-over-curve** `AoC = (1/B_ep) ∫₀^{B_ep} B(c) dc` over the episode budget `B_ep`; validity rate; **robustness** `R` = locked sensitivity statistic (e.g. min quality retained across prespecified sensitivity replicates, formula at Lock 1). **Pareto frontier** computed **within a comparable domain** (same task panel + evaluator version + endpoint) over (final B, total cost, R). Precedence: frontier membership = evidence quality; saturation/pruning = allocation status — a nondominated-but-saturated trajectory stays frontier-listed but allocation-paused; neither label overwrites the other.
    49	
    50	**0.4.4 Origins & generation.** Sol-xhigh proposes candidates under the frozen protocol (§0.1); each declares exactly one node-operation/origin:
    51	- `new` (0 parents; opens unexplored direction) · `refinement` (exactly 1 parent; mutate/extend a frontier or winner artifact) · `underexplored_extension` (1+ parents in a thin-evidence direction) · `combination` (≥2 parents; each contribution stated; legality of each element checked; revalidated from scratch; no score inheritance) · `repair` (≥1 parent + cited failure node ID + named fix).
    52	Runner rejects (ledgered, fed back next round): outside locked spaces; duplicate `config_id` without replication authorization; nonexistent parents/evidence; `avoid_repeating` repeats without `repair` justification; cyclic or version-incompatible lineage.
    53	
    54	**0.4.5 Selection = executable policy over prefix-recomputed state.** Proposal (sol-xhigh: what *could* run + priorities as inputs) ≠ selection (versioned `policy(episode_view, legal_actions, remaining_budget) → action`). **Legal actions are recomputed at each step from the revealed prefix** using frozen transition/pruning rules — historical legal-action sequences are audit records, never counterfactual state for challengers. Keep/prune: frontier trajectories refinable; allocation-paused (saturated) directions leave the legal set until a frozen reactivation rule fires; `fragile` trajectories refinable only via prespecified robustness diagnostics. All recomputation is deterministic from the episode view (replayable bit-for-bit given the same revealed outcomes).
    55	
    56	**0.4.6 Dreaming: replay objective, ρ, exact context, common support.**
    57	- **ρ is a budget-control parameter** (as in the paper): replay evaluates each policy at operating budgets `ρ·B_ep` for ρ ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1}. **Replay objective (complete formula frozen at Lock 1; default):** per episode e and ρ, `J_e(π,ρ) = B_e(ρ·B_ep)` (best valid quality attained within that budget; invalid nodes contribute cost, no quality; zero-attainment ⇒ 0). Policy score `J(π) = Σ_ρ w_ρ · mean_e J_e(π,ρ)` with locked weights `w_ρ` (default uniform), ties broken by lower mean cost-to-τ then incumbent preference. Costs are measured ledger costs (§0.4.1 ancestry rule); endpoints' signs/scales per contract.
    58	- **Exact context (operational):** refinement generation is **parent-local by construction** — the generator's inputs are exactly its canonical manifest (parent artifact(s) + frozen instruction + episode-view snapshot ID + budget token). A recorded child replays **iff** the challenger's episode state reproduces that manifest hash exactly; **any differing input ⇒ `unsupported`**, never approximated.
    59	- **Common support (frozen at Lock 1):** comparison set = frozen list of episodes (world × panel × seed); **coverage** = supported decisions / all decision points; **eligibility threshold** (default: ≥ 80% supported decisions per policy and ≥ 8 complete episodes per policy, else replay is *inadmissible as a screen* for that comparison → fresh rollouts decide); an unsupported action **terminates the episode as `unsupported`** — its quality is excluded from means, its cost counts in coverage reporting, and it can **never** count as a cheap successful stop. Policies compared **paired on identical episode sets**; aggregation weights locked; support coverage always reported beside scores.
    60	- Both arms share the same first round **E0** (starting policy; copied read-only; its production cost charged per Phase 5 allocation — copying is not free).
    61	
    62	**0.4.7 Audit views.** `status --trajectories`: lineage DAG (all parents), direction board (4-field statuses), frontier data, generation-over-generation table, allocation-paused list + reasons, coverage reports. Derived deterministically from ledgers; ledger wins on disagreement (= view bug).
    63	
    64	### 0.5 Generation protocol (adapted from the business LOOP, hardened)
    65	
    66	Modeled on `~/bufferright/business/data/research/paths/LOOP/` (48 generations; "Sol xhigh proposes, Sol high reviews"; score trails 54→76→92). Translation: sleeve pool → analysis-family pool; blend → `combination`; PREREG hash → SPEC hash; forward ledger → frozen predictions; anchor → evaluator-compatibility suite (below). **Two separate lifecycles** (never mixed): **(S) scientific-artifact generations** and **(P) policy generations**, each with own SPEC, screen, battery, denominators, trial counters.
    67	
    68	**0.5.1 Anatomy (`generations/{s,p}genNNN/`, never reused/overwritten).**
    69	1. **SPEC.md + SHA-256 locked in ledger before any run:** discriminating question, candidate families, screen definition (incl. whether it is a *necessary-condition* screen — §0.5.2), battery definition, pass bar, stop rules, reserved battery budget. Amendments = new versioned files; locked-science amendments follow new-study-ID rule.
    70	2. **Enumerate** from the pool under frozen protocol; origins+parents per §0.4.4; legality validated.
    71	3. **Screen (ranking only, labeled approximate).** Screen-positives are never claims.
    72	4. **Recombine/refine winners** (`combination`/`refinement`); re-enter at screen; no score inheritance.
    73	5. **Battery (decisive):** (S) full frozen evaluator + validity + matched controls + ablations on fresh development rollouts; (P) Phase 4/5 procedure (replay screen → fresh paired rollouts incl. incumbent). Battery budget is **reserved before screening**; if ceilings intervene, unfinished batteries are `PENDING`, never silently dropped.
    74	6. **Verdicts are scoped to what was tested:** `ADVANCES` · `FAILS-BATTERY (tested finalists: n/N, with numbers)` · `FAMILY-CLOSED` (allowed **only** with a proven necessary-condition screen or exhaustive battery — §0.5.2; else untested members remain explicitly open) · `INVALID` (defective record preserved, corrected in a new version) · `BLOCKED-DATA` (no proxy substitution without claim downgrade) · `PENDING`.
    75	7. **Sol-high review → verify → fix → re-review to ≥ 90**, per-issue dispositions (`CONFIRMED+FIXED` / `ACCEPTED with claim downgrade` / `REJECTED with evidence`).
    76	8. **`GENERATIONS.md` entry:** numbers for every finalist incl. failures, lessons, next mandate, stop-rule checks (leakage ⇒ dependent lines cancelled), and **multi-counter trial accounting** (cumulative, separate: unique configs, attempts, replications, hypothesis inspections, policy candidates, replay episodes, batteries — scientific vs engineering adaptation never merged into one total; no count substitutes for confirmatory procedure).
    77	
    78	**0.5.2 Screening honesty.** A screen justifies exhaustive rejection **only** if passing it is a mathematically necessary condition for passing the battery (proof stated in SPEC, e.g. battery metric is a monotone refinement bounded by the screen metric). Otherwise verdicts read "no *tested finalist* passes" and screen-negative members stay listed as untested. Cheapest-falsification probes run as one fixed SPEC with no variants; failure closes only the tested specification, with family-level closure requiring the necessary-condition argument.
    79	
    80	**0.5.3 Evaluator versioning (anchor is not certification).** Scores from different evaluator versions are **never pooled** in one comparison/replay pool. The frozen baseline "anchor" reproduction is a smoke check only. Any evaluator change requires: compatibility suite (rankings + validity decisions on a frozen probe set), explicit invalidation list for affected results, and — if substantive — the new-study/new-evidence procedure. Versions preserved side-by-side.
    81	
    82	**0.5.4 Matched controls & post-hoc rule.** A new ingredient is credited only vs a same-construction control lacking it; unmatched construction changes are disclosed as non-decomposable. Post-hoc discoveries (e.g. control beats hypothesis) are flagged post-hoc, may be frozen for forward judging, never promotable on consumed data.
    83	
    84	**0.5.5 Generation boundary (end-to-end).** Deterministic scores/frontier/episode views → sol-xhigh direction summary + ranked proposal (keep / recombine-with-rationale / repair / open / pause, all ID-cited) → policy code executes selection under budget → runs → extraction + predicate verification → sol-high ≥ 90 → ledger + trace-pool append.
    85	
    86	### 0.6 Autonomy, unstick protocol, campaign limits & terminal determination
    87	- **Default = continue autonomously** through failures/negative results, generation after generation, **within campaign allocations** (Lock 1: per-phase + total wall/nodes/model-calls, inside §0.2 ceilings). Legitimate stops: hard authorization gate (credential/spending/required file), allocation/ceiling exhaustion, explicit user stop, no-progress determination (below), terminal determination.
    88	- **Unstick (sol-xhigh):** on stall (no obvious legal action, unexpected gate failure, all directions paused/dead/blocked, contradictory results, same failure twice) invoke sol-xhigh with the state packet (STATUS, episode views, frontier, last 3 generation entries, blocker + attempts) for ranked unblocks + cheapest discriminating experiment + draft next SPEC. Verify vs locked contract (sol cannot authorize contract/ceiling/final-access/standard changes); ledger consultation+disposition; **unstick calls are charged costs** (Phase 5). Max 3 consultation rounds per blocker, then smallest legal evidence-gathering action. Under a frozen comparison, unstick input is restricted to that arm's admissible history (§0.1 freeze).
    89	- **No-progress / negative-EV rule (prevents indefinite retries, spec §14):** if K consecutive generations (K at Lock 1, default 3) produce no frontier movement, no new identifiable question, and no gate progress, sol-xhigh + lead jointly assess; concurrence ⇒ admissible **non-complete terminal determination** (`INCONCLUSIVE`/`BLOCKED` as applicable) with unmet gates + smallest next step. Global retry limits per failure mode also frozen at Lock 1.
    90	- **Terminal determination (sol-xhigh judges, evidence rules):** `COMPLETE_*` requires sol-xhigh's written gate-by-gate (C1–C13) certification over the full evidence package, score ≥ 90, zero unresolved high/critical — **in addition to** the evidence, never instead of it; it cannot waive gates. A **pre-reserved terminal-review budget slice** (Lock 1) guarantees the call is affordable; if it still cannot run, the executor may mechanically checkpoint and report `BLOCKED`/`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` honestly — but **positive completion always requires the sol-xhigh determination**. Non-complete determinations list exact unmet gates; consumed Layer-B/C evidence stays consumed (Phases 5–6).
    91	
    92	---

     1	# GOAL — Cancer Dependency RSI (locked goal statement)
     2	
     3	> **Goal-tool objective (copy verbatim into `create_goal`):**
     4	> Discover and independently support a previously unreported, precisely bounded explanation of when molecularly defined cancer dependencies persist or fail across measured culture environments — and prove, under a fixed scientific evaluator and matched total resources, that a learned research policy beats strong fixed exploration by a preregistered practical margin. Do not stop at a plan, scaffold, leaderboard, or narrative: run real experiments on verified public data, complete at least two genuine policy-improvement cycles with faithful replay and fresh rollouts, evaluate one locked claim family once on independent external outcomes, and ship a reproducible evidence atlas, manuscript, and reproducibility package. If any gate fails, report the exact unmet gates — never reinterpret insufficient evidence as success.
     5	
     6	- **Project:** `cancer-dependency-rsi/` (isolated under this workspace)
     7	- **Specification:** `cancer_rsi_handoff.txt` (28 Oct 2026) — the research specification; this file is its locked goal projection. On conflict, the specification wins.
     8	- **Execution plan:** `PLAN.md`
     9	- **Status / resume:** `cancer-dependency-rsi/STATUS.md` (append-only ledgers alongside it)
    10	
    11	## The ambition (all five, not any one)
    12	
    13	1. **A new explanatory finding.** At least one previously unreported, precisely bounded, externally supported result — a positive conditional dependency rule, a convincing refutation of a meaningful dependency claim, or an informative bound that changes how a target should be interpreted. It must resolve a specific predeclared uncertainty. A better benchmark score, a rediscovered association, or a nonsignificant p-value does not count.
    14	2. **A complete evidence atlas.** Every eligible gene in the controlled environmental panel (250 selected genes) with support, contradiction, heterogeneity, uncertainty, missing evidence, and every exclusion with its reason.
    15	3. **A defensible environment→transfer connection.** Measured environmental sensitivity linked to transfer behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort, beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline — only where the evidence supports it, with distinct evidence labels for intervention vs. association.
    16	4. **A real policy-improvement loop.** Executable policies, faithful historical replay on recorded outcomes only, fresh-rollout promotion tests, incumbent retention on failure, ≥2 genuine cycles, and a final frozen-policy comparison on reserved panels against fixed exploration and a fixed-allocation/random strategy.
    17	5. **Demonstrated efficiency + a review-ready package.** Preregistered practical improvement under matched total resources including learning overhead (default target: ≥20% lower total measured cost to a predeclared quality threshold, one-sided 95% lower bound meeting the margin), plus manuscript and full reproducibility package with all failures, limitations, and costs disclosed.
    18	
    19	**Stretch (not a substitute):** multiple convergent findings across adequately represented lineages/pathways. Never trade one strong finding for a quota of weak associations; never force claims about sparsely represented cancer types.
    20	
    21	## Scientific question (to be narrowed at the identifiability gate)
    22	
    23	Does measured sensitivity to culture conditions (medium, growth format), together with genotype and tumor state, explain failures of dependency transfer from conventional cancer models to patient-derived organoids beyond lineage and technical variation alone?
    24	
    25	- **Direct environmental estimand:** within-model change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and B holding the other measured condition fixed; medium and format analyzed separately where the design permits; original assay units for within-study paired contrasts.
    26	- **Transfer estimand:** extent to which a prespecified genotype / tumor-state / context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest baseline.
    27	- Generic cell-line→organoid prediction is already studied (October 2026 transferability study, 20 genes) and **cannot** be the novelty claim. Recovering published lipid/adhesion/state findings is a control, not a discovery.
    28	
    29	## Non-negotiables
    30	
    31	- Scientific evaluator, splits, practical margins, multiplicity control, and success criteria are **frozen before** policy optimization and before final evaluation. The policy may change branching, prioritization, refinement, and stopping — never labels, metrics, margins, or success criteria.
    32	- Three evaluation layers stay separate: **(A)** development panels, **(B)** reserved policy-evaluation panels, **(C)** final external biological evaluation (independently filtered Sanger cohort preferred). Final outcomes are never a replay reward and are touched only after the second lock commits hypotheses, predictions, artifacts, and inferential code.
    33	- Count independent biological models/donors correctly. Genes, guides, wells, cultures, and replicates are not patients. Broad Chronos effects and Sanger fitness scores are different measurement systems — never subtracted directly.
    34	- Never reward small p-values, discovery counts, or persuasive narratives. Never invent outcomes for unexplored actions; unsupported replay actions stay unsupported. Synthetic data tests code only, never biology.
    35	- All scientific fitting, simulation, evaluation, and data processing run locally on this laptop (Apple M5 Max, 18 cores, 128 GB RAM, macOS 26.6); hosted frontier-model reasoning is used only through the already-authorized host/codex interface. No GPU, ≤2 CPU-heavy jobs, research memory ≤ min(8 GiB, 60% RAM), 10-min default exploratory timeout, ≤20 GiB downloads, first pilot ≤8 h, additional metered API spend cap = $0 unless the user sets a numeric cap. Campaign allocations are fixed at Lock 1 within these ceilings; ceilings never auto-increase.
    36	- Separate status fields, always: `scientific_discovery`, `prediction_improvement`, `policy_improvement`. One never establishes another.
    37	
    38	## Completion — all 13 gates (C1–C13) must hold
    39	
    40	| Gate | Requirement (abbreviated; specification §12 is authoritative) |
    41	|---|---|
    42	| C1 | Current novelty audit: precise unresolved question, distinguished from Aug resources + Oct transfer study |
    43	| C2 | Legitimate public data only; full provenance, licensing, ID crosswalks, reproducible retrieval/cache |
    44	| C3 | Identifiable paired contrast / conditional relationship in a named population; correct independent-unit counts |
    45	| C4 | Contract, eligibility, splits, baselines, margins, evaluator, confirmatory procedures frozen before optimization/final eval |
    46	| C5 | Baselines reproduced closely enough to establish direction, cohort integrity, measurement interpretation |
    47	| C6 | ≥1 previously unreported explanatory finding passes frozen criteria + external tests (refutation/bound can qualify) |
    48	| C7 | Evidence atlas covers all eligible panel genes; every exclusion listed; evidence types distinguished |
    49	| C8 | ≥2 real policy cycles: executable challengers, faithful replay, fresh rollouts, retained failures, final reserved-panel comparison |
    50	| C9 | Learned policy meets preregistered practical-improvement criterion vs strongest fixed baseline, end-to-end incl. overhead |
    51	| C10 | Conclusions survive multiplicity correction, uncertainty analysis, ablations, adversarial checks; invariance needs positive + equivalence evidence |
    52	| C11 | Clean reproduction from frozen artifacts within tolerances; no stochastic LLM rerun required |
    53	| C12 | Manuscript + machine-readable evidence, manifests, histories, versions, measured resources, failures, limitations, reproduce/resume commands |
    54	| C13 | First meaningful end-to-end cycle actually executed (plan/scaffold/mock is insufficient) |
    55	
    56	## Terminal states (use exactly one; list unmet gates otherwise)
    57	
    58	`COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_DISCOVERY` · `COMPLETE_SUPPORTED_REFUTATION_OR_BOUND` (both require every relevant gate incl. C9) · `SCIENTIFIC_RESULT_WITHOUT_RSI_EVIDENCE` · `RSI_RESULT_WITHOUT_SCIENTIFIC_DISCOVERY` · `INCONCLUSIVE` · `BLOCKED` · `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED`
    59	
    60	A completed computational campaign is not a completed scientific goal. Insufficient power is not equivalence. A polished manuscript is not a discovery.
    61	
    62	## Model routing (user-mandated)
    63	
    64	- **Review / adversarial audit of everything:** `gpt-6.1-sol` at `high` reasoning via `codex exec` — must enumerate low/medium/high/critical issues and score 0–100; verified issues are fixed; re-review until score ≥ 90.
    65	- **Generating new generations** (policy challengers, hypothesis/analysis generations): `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh` reasoning. Sol-xhigh works from the full tried history via the locked context/retrieval contract (exhaustive history index + episode-admissible catalog/frontier views + ID-cited full records, PLAN §0.1/§0.4), picks the most promising trajectories to keep, recombines 2+ recorded parents into new candidates with machine-checked DAG lineage, refines winners, repairs recoverable failures, and opens unexplored directions (PLAN §0.4–0.5, after Google Dream-RSI and the `~/bufferright/business` 48-generation LOOP). The whole generator protocol is frozen per comparison; executed selection remains versioned policy code under the fixed evaluator — sol proposes, the policy disposes.
    66	- Every model call is ledgered (model ID, effort, prompt hash, tokens when exposed, wall/CPU time, cost or `unknown`). Unknown material cost ⇒ C9 all-in savings cannot be certified; the gate stays unresolved rather than assumed.
    67	
    68	## Autonomy & terminal determination (user-mandated)
    69	
    70	- **Never idle, never self-certify.** Work continues autonomously, generation after generation, through failures and negative results, within Lock-1 campaign allocations. When stuck, consult `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh` with the full state packet for ranked unblock options + cheapest discriminating experiments (PLAN §0.6 unstick protocol), then keep working on the best legal option. Stopping is legitimate only at a hard authorization gate, allocation/budget exhaustion, explicit user stop, the locked no-progress/negative-EV determination, or terminal determination — indefinite retries are forbidden (spec §14).
    71	- **Sol-xhigh determines satisfaction.** The goal counts as satisfied only when `gpt-6.1-sol` at `xhigh`, reviewing the complete evidence package, determines gate-by-gate (C1–C13) that a `COMPLETE_*` state is documented — score ≥ 90, zero unresolved high/critical objections. Its determination supplements the evidence; it can never waive a gate or substitute for missing artifacts. Unmet gates become the next mandate list while allocations remain; when they don't, sol-xhigh certifies the honest non-complete terminal state + exact unmet gates (a pre-reserved terminal-review slice guarantees affordability; if the call still cannot run, mechanical `BLOCKED`/`BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` checkpointing is permitted, but positive completion always requires sol-xhigh).
    72	
    73	## Definition of done for this goal
    74	
    75	Terminal state is one of the two `COMPLETE_*` states with documentary evidence for C1–C13, a clean-room reproduction passing within tolerances, and the first-substantive-report contents (verified data, identifiable question, baseline result, real experiments, replay support, retained/promoted policy, measured resources, next discriminating experiment) all superseded by final locked results. Anything less is reported under its honest terminal state with the precise unmet gates and the smallest concrete next step.

    85	The starting literature establishes several constraints.
    86	
    87	- The Broad resource reports 147 genome-scale screens, while the Sanger resource reports screens across 162 organoids. These counts are not automatically disjoint biological samples.
    88	- The smaller controlled Broad environmental experiment has 250 deliberately selected test genes and 18 screens across only seven biological models. It includes seven matched format contrasts and five matched medium contrasts, with possible repeated models. Verify the exact design in the source.
    89	- The 250 genes were selected partly because they differed between model environments. They are not a representative genome-wide sample.
    90	- The October 2026 transferability study already examines expression-based transfer from conventional models to Broad and Sanger organoids for 20 selected genes. Generic 2D-to-organoid transfer is not the new contribution.
    91	- Broad's temporary NextGen manuscript dataset was incorporated into DepMap Public 26Q3. Staff direct users to the integrated CRISPRGeneEffect.csv and relevant model metadata. Reverify the current route rather than inventing a file ID or assuming old instructions still work.
    92	- Sanger processed fitness measurements and Broad Chronos gene effects are different measurement systems. Their absolute values are not interchangeable.
    93	- Historical releases, a current integrated release, and independently produced laboratory data are different forms of evidence. Reprocessing the same observations is not biological replication.
    94	
    95	Resolve downloads through current official manifests. Record source URL, release or commit, retrieval date, license, byte size, and checksum. Do not invent direct download URLs or accession metadata. Use official alternative routes if a public portal blocks unattended retrieval. Do not bypass a challenge or access control.
    96	
    97	Build around public processed observations. Raw sequencing is unnecessary. If a proposed analysis requires paired observations that are not actually released, identify that limitation before implementing the model.

Distinguish that outcome leakage from covariate-only joint preprocessing, which must be disclosed as transductive feature preprocessing with appropriately limited claims.    149	6 - Scientific estimands and research contract
   150	
   151	Start from two connected estimands.
   152	
   153	The direct environmental estimand is the change in a gene's measured dependency between environment A and environment B within the same biological model, while holding the other measured culture condition fixed. Analyze medium and growth format separately where the design permits.
   154	
   155	The transfer estimand is the extent to which a prespecified genotype, tumor-state, or context relationship predicts dependency behavior in an independently filtered organoid cohort beyond lineage and the strongest appropriate baseline.
   156	
   157	Use original documented assay units for paired within-study contrasts. Use justified ranking, calibration, or outcome-specific comparisons for cross-study evidence.
   158	
   159	For each environmental claim, report the dependency in both conditions, all individual paired changes, uncertainty, and heterogeneity. A near-zero average can conceal large opposing effects.
   160	
   161	Do not call a dependency invariant because a difference is nonsignificant. If claiming equivalence, define a scientifically justified equivalence margin before confirmatory evaluation and require the relevant interval to fall inside that margin. If no defensible margin exists, report continuous effects and uncertainty without an invariance label.
   162	
   163	A stable absence of dependency is not a stable cancer vulnerability. Establish that the dependency itself is meaningful in the environments covered by a positive claim.
   164	
   165	Do not interpret a predictive expression feature as a causal mediator without identifying assumptions and suitable evidence. Environmental intervention evidence and cross-cohort biomarker associations must retain distinct evidence labels.
   166	
   167	Carry uncertainty and shared-model dependence in estimated environmental sensitivity into downstream transfer analyses using a justified measurement-error, resampling, or sensitivity analysis. Do not treat noisy estimated sensitivities as exact independent inputs. Report conclusions that change materially across plausible environmental-effect estimates.
   168	
   169	During initial development, choose and justify the precise primary endpoint, practical effect margin, hypothesis family, baseline set, uncertainty procedure, and sensitivity analyses. Then freeze them before policy optimization.
   170	
   171	The research contract must include the following.
   172	
   173	- Exact question and falsifiable alternatives.
   174	- Intended population, environments, targets, and generalization claim.
   175	- Eligibility and exclusion rules.
   176	- Data and identifier versions.
   177	- Development, policy-evaluation, and final external partitions.
   178	- Feature engineering and transformation rules.
   179	- Primary and secondary endpoints with fixed direction.
   180	- Minimum practical effects or equivalence margins and their justification.
   181	- Multiplicity control and uncertainty methods.
   182	- Baseline definitions.
   183	- Invalid-result rules.
   184	- Success, failure, inconclusive, and budget-exhaustion conditions.
   185	
   186	The initial lock fixes the permitted hypothesis and transformation spaces, partitions, evaluator, baseline definitions, practical margins, and inferential rules. Adaptive development may select hypotheses, features, hyperparameters, and fitted artifacts within those spaces. A second lock commits the exact selected claim family, predictions, and artifacts before external evaluation.
   187	
   188	Hash this contract, evaluator code, and split manifests. Changing a locked scientific contract creates a new study identifier and requires appropriate new evaluation evidence.
   189	
   190	
   191	7 - Novelty and biological scope
   192	
   193	Maintain a claim ledger throughout the project.
   194	
   195	For each proposed claim, record the nearest prior result, which part is already established, the precise remaining uncertainty, evidence needed to resolve it, and the consequence if the claim is supported or refuted.
   196	
   197	Read the Broad environmental analyses, the Sanger biomarker analyses, and the October transfer study closely. Their reported lipid, adhesion, state, and dependency findings are controls or prior evidence. Recovering them is not a new discovery.
   198	
   199	Use current primary literature, author data, and official documentation. Date the search and preserve queries and important exclusions. Absence of an exact search hit is not proof that a claim is new.
   200	
   201	Choose a bounded hypothesis family before final evaluation. Consider gene classes with measured environmental sensitivity and adequate overlap, not an unconstrained scan of every feature until a small p-value appears.
   202

   204	
   205	The required scientific contribution must be more specific than weak organoid transfer, better average correlation, or successful agent automation.

Any reproduction requiring final external outcomes must occur after the final hypotheses, predictions, artifacts, and inferential code are committed, alongside the final evaluation. Those results cannot inform revisions to the tested study.

Do not manufacture an independent environmental test by splitting genes from the same seven models.    256

   264	10 - Implement the actual RSI loop
   265	
   266	Keep the base scientific generator, its model version and prompting contract, data contract, and evaluator fixed during a policy comparison.
   267	
   268	Make the editable policy an executable module that receives observed history, legal actions, and remaining resources, and selects an action.
   269	
   270	Legal actions may include opening a predefined analysis family, choosing an existing branch, refining a particular artifact, running a permitted diagnostic, and stopping.
   271	
   272	The policy may alter branching, prioritization, exploration versus refinement, and stopping. It cannot change labels, splits, metrics, practical margins, multiplicity correction, or success criteria.
   273	
   274	A narrative reflection is not a policy improvement unless it changes executed decisions.
   275	
   276	Record each discovery node's immutable parent, exact artifact, input context hash, data and split hashes, evaluator hash, seeds, result, validity diagnostics, failures, model calls, and measured costs.
   277	
   278	Historical replay may reveal only previously executed artifacts and their actual recorded outcomes. It must not use a model to invent outcomes for unexplored analyses.
   279	
   280	A reordered branch may alter the context seen by a generator. Make refinement depend on an immutable parent and fixed context, or require an exact context match before replaying a recorded child. Do not assume a recorded child represents an arbitrary counterfactual generation.
   281	
   282	The replay policy sees only the outcomes revealed along its trajectory. Do not expose the full tree's future rewards.
   283	
   284	Mark unsupported replay actions unsupported. Do not assign them fake scores or treat a surrogate prediction as a measured outcome. Compare policies on common support, report support coverage, and use replay as a screening tool.
   285	
   286	Perform the following cycle.
   287	
   288	1. Run the initial fixed policy on real development tasks.
   289	2. Preserve the evaluated discovery trees as replay worlds.
   290	3. Generate at least three executable policy challengers plus the unchanged incumbent.
   291	4. Replay them under the same resource constraints and support rules.
   292	5. Select a challenger with the frozen selection rule.
   293	6. Test the challenger in fresh development rollouts against fixed exploration using the same generator and evaluator.
   294	7. Promote only when the preregistered criterion is met.
   295	8. Retain the incumbent when challengers fail.
   296	9. Add only development histories and repeat.
   297	
   298	Complete at least two genuine cycles before the final policy comparison if the authorized resources and valid tasks permit. Two cycles do not require two promotions. An unchanged incumbent is a valid experimental outcome.
   299	
   300	After development, freeze the best admissible policy and evaluate it on the reserved policy panels. Compare it with the original fixed exploration strategy and at least one simple fixed-allocation or random-selection strategy.
   301	
   302	
   303	11 - What counts as demonstrated policy improvement
   304	
   305	Predeclare one primary operating point or cost-to-quality endpoint. Also report complete scientific-quality-versus-cost curves.
   306	
   307	Use the same generator, evaluator, initial artifacts, information permissions, and common resource ceilings for all policies.
   308	
   309	Charge policy rewriting, replay, failed jobs, promotion trials, and extra model calls to the improving system. Permit fixed baselines to spend the corresponding budget on scientific search.
   310	
   311	Report both end-to-end performance including learning overhead and deployment-only performance with learning costs shown separately.
   312	
   313	The ambitious engineering target is at least 20 percent lower total measured API expenditure to reach a predeclared valid scientific-quality threshold, under the same CPU ceiling and information constraints, compared with the strongest fixed baseline. This is a project target, not a forecast.
   314	
   315	Set the quality threshold from development evidence before final policy evaluation. It must be meaningful and attainable by competent baselines, not chosen after observing which policy wins.
   316	
   317	Use paired blocked comparisons where appropriate and report uncertainty. For the default 20 percent savings goal, require the prespecified one-sided 95 percent lower confidence bound on savings to meet the 20 percent practical margin. A point estimate above 20 percent with a lower bound merely above zero supports some improvement but does not meet that ambitious margin. Freeze how failures to reach the quality threshold within budget are treated as censored outcomes. Do not convert nonattainment into invented exact costs. Random seeds quantify execution variability, not independent biological replication.
   318	
   319	If API expenditure is not the dominant or measurable resource, choose a justified alternative primary resource endpoint during the initial contract stage, explain it, and lock it before optimization. All other material resources must still be measured or strictly matched. A CPU-only advantage or token proxy cannot satisfy C9 while material host-model costs remain unknown and unmatched. In that case retain an unresolved cost gate and avoid claiming all-in savings. Define an equally explicit practical improvement margin and uncertainty requirement for any alternative endpoint. Do not switch endpoints after seeing final results.
   320	
   321	If faithful replay has inadequate support, preserve that finding and use fresh rollouts to assess the proposed policy. Label the method accurately as a Dream-RSI-inspired adaptation and disclose departures.
   322	
   323	Do not call the system recursively self-improving merely because more compute improved a reused validation score.

   327

   330	C1 - A current novelty audit identifies a precise unresolved cancer dependency question and distinguishes it from the August resources and October transfer study.

   333

   339

Mere nonsignificance, a rediscovered association, or a better exploratory score does not qualify.
   341	
   342	C7 - The evidence atlas covers all eligible genes in the controlled panel and lists every exclusion. Every entry distinguishes dependency strength, environmental change, uncertainty, heterogeneity, biomarker evidence, transfer evidence, and prior knowledge.
   343

   347

   350	C11 - A clean reproduction run regenerates the primary estimates, figures, evidence decisions, and policy comparison within documented tolerances from frozen artifacts. It must not require rerunning stochastic LLM search to reproduce the reported analysis.
   351	
   352	C12 - The package includes a manuscript for expert review, machine-readable evidence, source and data manifests, all experiment histories, model and policy versions, actual resource measurements, failures, limitations, and exact reproduce and resume commands.
   353	
   354	C13 - The first meaningful end-to-end research cycle has actually executed. A plan, scaffold, mock evaluator, or hypothetical example is insufficient.
   355	
   356	Seek multiple convergent findings across adequately represented lineages and pathways as an additional scientific stretch target. Do not replace one strong finding with a quota of weak associations. Do not force claims about sparsely represented cancer types.
   357	
   358	Keep scientific discovery, prediction improvement, and policy improvement as separate status fields. One does not establish the others.
   359	
   360	
   361	13 - Required artifacts and commands
   362	
   363	Create a compact repository with equivalent artifacts to the following. Choose actual file formats suited to the content.
   364	
   365	- README.md with scope, installation, exact commands, and current limitations.
   366	- GOAL.md with the locked goal and completion criteria.

   371	- Data adapters, baseline models, a fixed evaluator, policy modules, a faithful replay engine, a local runner, and a budget wrapper.
   372	- An append-only experiment ledger and immutable discovery artifacts.
   373	- Frozen final predictions, scientific models, policies, and checksums.
   374	- An evidence atlas in a machine-readable table.

   380
